     1	"""All the process that can be run using nox.
     2	
     3	The nox run are build in isolated environment that will be stored in .nox. to force the venv update, remove the .nox/xxx folder.
     4	"""
     5	
     6	import nox
     7	
     8	
     9	@nox.session(reuse_venv=True)
    10	def lint(session):
    11	    """Apply the pre-commits."""
    12	    session.install("pre-commit")
    13	    session.run("pre-commit", "run", "--a", *session.posargs)
    14	
    15	
    16	@nox.session(reuse_venv=True)
    17	def test(session):
    18	    """Run all the test using the environment varialbe of the running machine."""
    19	    session.install(".[test]")
    20	    test_files = session.posargs or ["tests"]
    21	    session.run("pytest", "--color=yes", "--cov", "--cov-report=html", *test_files)
    22	
    23	
    24	@nox.session(reuse_venv=True)
    25	def bin(session):
    26	    """Run all the bin methods to validate the conda recipe."""
    27	    session.install(".")
    28	    session.run("module_deploy", "--help")
    29	    session.run("module_factory", "--help")
    30	    session.run("module_l10n", "--help")
    31	    session.run("module_theme", "--help")
    32	    session.run("module_venv", "--help")
    33	    session.run("activate_venv", "--help")
    34	    session.run("sepal_ipyvuetify", "--help")
    35	
    36	
    37	@nox.session(reuse_venv=True)
    38	def docs(session):
    39	    """Build the documentation."""
    40	    session.install(".[doc]")
    41	    # patch version in nox instead of pyproject to avoid blocking conda releases

<#sep#>   - For \(x = 4\):
     \[
     4! + 1 = 24 + 1 = 25
     \]
     \(25\) is a perfect square, and \(25 = 5^2\). Therefore, \((x, y) = (4, 5)\) is a solution.

   - For \(x = 5\):
     \[
     5! + 1 = 120 + 1 = 121
     \]
     \(121\) is a perfect square, and \(121 = 11^2\). Therefore, \((x, y) = (5, 11)\) is a solution.

   - For \(x = 6\):
     \[
     6! + 1 = 720 + 1 = 721
     \]
     \(721\) is not a perfect square.

   - For \(x = 7\):
     \[
     7! + 1 = 5040 + 1 = 5041
     \]
     \(5041\) is a perfect square, and \(5041 = 71^2\). Therefore, \((x, y) = (7, 71)\) is a solution.

2. **Considering larger values of \(x\):**
   - For \(x \geq 8\), \(x!\) grows very rapidly. We need to check if \(x! + 1\) can be a perfect square.
   - For \(x = 8\):
     \[
     8! + 1 = 40320 + 1 = 40321
     \]
     \(40321\) is not a perfect square (as shown by checking its square root).

   - For \(x = 9\):
     \[
     9! + 1 = 362880 + 1 = 362881
     \]
     \(362881\) is not a perfect square (as shown by checking its square root).

   - For \(x = 10\):
     \[
     10! + 1 = 3628800 + 1 = 3628801
     \]
     \(3628801\) is not a perfect square (as shown by checking its square root).

   - As \(x\) increases, \(x!\) grows extremely fast, making \(x! + 1\) unlikely to be a perfect square. Therefore, no further solutions are expected.

   316	    def __init__(
   317	        self,
   318	        msg: str = "",
   319	        type_: str = "info",
   320	        id_: str = "",
   321	        persistent: bool = True,
   322	        **kwargs,
   323	    ) -> None:
   324	        r"""Custom Snackbar widget to display messages as a banner in module App.
   325	
   326	        Args:
   327	            msg: Message to display in application banner. default to nothing
   328	            type\_: Used to display an appropiate banner color. fallback to "info".
   329	            id_: unique banner identificator.
   330	            persistent: Whether to close automatically based on the lenght of message (False) or make it indefinitely open (True). Overridden if timeout duration is set.
   331	            kwargs (optional): any parameter from a v.Alert. If set, 'vertical' and 'top' will be overwritten.
   332	        """
   333	        # compute the type and default to "info" if it's not existing
   334	        type_ = set_type(type_)
   335	
   336	        # create the closing btn
   337	        self.btn_close = v.Btn(
   338	            small=True, text=True, children=[ms.widgets.banner.close]
   339	        )
   340	
   341	        # compute timeout based on the persistent and timeout parameter
   342	        computed_timeout = 0 if persistent is True else self.get_timeout(msg)
   343	
   344	        kwargs.setdefault("color", type_)
   345	        kwargs.setdefault("transition", "scroll-x-transition")
   346	        kwargs["attributes"] = {"id": id_}
   347	        kwargs.setdefault("v_model", True)
   348	        kwargs.setdefault("timeout", computed_timeout)
   349	        kwargs["top"] = True
   350	        kwargs["vertical"] = True
   351	        kwargs["children"] = [msg] + [self.btn_close]
   352	        kwargs["class_"] = "mb-1"
   353	
   354	        super().__init__(**kwargs)
   355	
   356	        self.btn_close.on_event("click", self.close)
   357	
   358	    def close(self, *args) -> None:
   359	        """Close button event to close snackbar alert."""
   360	        self.v_model = False  # mypy: ignore-errors
   361	
   362	        return
   363	
   364	    def get_timeout(self, text: str) -> int:
   365	        """Calculate timeout in miliseconds to read the message.
   366	
   367	        Args:
   368	            text: the text displayed in the banner to adapt the duration of the timeout
   369	
   370	        Returns:
   371	            the duration of the timeout in milliseconds
   372	        """
   373	        wpm = 180  # readable words per minute
   374	        word_length = 5  # standardized number of chars in calculable word
   375	        words = len(text) / word_length
   376	        words_time = ((words / wpm) * 60) * 1000
   377	
   378	        delay = 1500  # milliseconds before user starts reading the notification
   379	        bonus = 1000  # extra time
   380	
   381	        return int(delay + words_time + bonus)
   382	
   383	    def set_btn(self, nb_banner: int) -> None:
   384	        """Change the btn display to inform the user on the number of banners in the queue.
   385	
   386	        Args:
   387	            nb_banner: the number of banners in the queue
   388	        """
   389	        # do not wrap ms.widget.banner. If you do it won't be recognized by the key-checker of the Translator
   390	        if nb_banner == 0:
   391	            txt = ms.widgets.banner.close
   392	        else:
   393	            txt = ms.widgets.banner.next.format(nb_banner)
   394	        self.btn_close.children = [txt]
   395	
   396	        return
   397

**Final Answer**
The value of \( f(23) \) is \boxed{133}.
</think>To solve for \( f(23) \), we need to determine the minimum number of red or blue edges in a complete graph with 23 vertices that guarantees the presence of either a red triangle or a blue 7-cycle.

### Step 1: Turán's Theorem for Red Triangle
Turán's theorem states that the maximum number of edges in a triangle-free graph (K₃-free) on \( n \) vertices is \( \left\lfloor \frac{n^2}{4} \right\rfloor \). For \( n = 23 \):
\[
\left\lfloor \frac{23^2}{4} \right\rfloor = \left\lfloor \frac{529}{4} \right\rfloor = \left\lfloor 132.25 \right\rfloor = 132
\]
Thus, any graph with more than 132 edges must contain a triangle. Therefore, if there are 133 red edges, a red triangle is guaranteed.

### Step 2: Ramsey Number for Blue 7-Cycle
The Ramsey number \( R(3, 7) \) is the smallest number such that any red-blue edge coloring of the complete graph on \( R(3, 7) \) vertices contains either a red triangle or a blue complete graph \( K_7 \). It is known that \( R(3, 7) = 23 \). This means any red-blue coloring of \( K_{23} \) contains either a red triangle or a blue \( K_7 \).

Since a blue \( K_7 \) contains a blue 7-cycle, any coloring of \( K_{<#sep#> six points into two disjoint triangles.

   5.5<#sep#> 7-Cycle
The Ramsey number \( R(3, 7) \) is the smallest number such that any red-blue edge coloring of the complete graph on \( R(3, 7) \) vertices contains either a red triangle or a blue complete graph \( K_7 \). It is known that \( R(3, 7) = 23 \). This means any red-blue coloring of \( K_{23} \) contains either a red triangle or a blue \( K_7 \).

Since a blue \( K_7 \) contains a blue 7-cycle, any coloring of \( K_{23} \) will have a blue 7-cycle if it contains a blue \( K_7 \).

### Step 3: Combining Results
Given the Ramsey number \( R(3, 7) = 23 \), any coloring of \( K_{23} \) must have either a red triangle or a blue 7-cycle. However, we need the minimum number of edges in one color to guarantee the structure.

- **Red Edges**: From Turán's theorem, 133 red edges guarantee a red triangle.
- **Blue Edges**: The Ramsey number \( R(3, 7) = 23 \) ensures that any coloring of \( K_{23} \) must have either a red triangle or a blue 7-cycle, but we need the minimum number of edges in one color to guarantee the structure.

Since \( R(3, 7) = 23 \) guarantees the structure, the critical threshold is determined by the Turán threshold for the red triangle, which is 133.

### Conclusion
Thus, the minimum number of edges required in either color to guarantee the presence of a red triangle or a blue 7-cycle is 133.

tool: Your thought has been logged.

assistant: ### 1.1-1.3 Problem Analysis

The issue is with the `Alert` widget's `update_progress` method in the `sepal_ui.sepalwidgets` module. Currently, developers must specify the `total` parameter every time they call `update_progress()`. The desired behavior is to set `total` once during initialization and then be able to call `update_progress()` with just the progress value in subsequent calls.

### 1.4 Steps to Reproduce
1. Import `sepalwidgets as sw`
2. Create an `Alert` instance: `alert = sw.Alert()`
3. Initialize progress with total: `alert.update_progress(0, "toto", total=10)`
4. Try updating progress without total: `alert.update_progress(i)` 
5. Observe that it fails or doesn't work correctly

### 1.5 Best Practices
- Store the `total` value as an instance variable after the first call
- Make the `total` parameter optional in subsequent calls
- Maintain backward compatibility with existing code
- Ensure the fix doesn't break existing functionality

Let's explore the repository structure and understand how to run tests.
